## 3-digit shorthand scanner follow-ups (2026-07-13)

- **Ship the `OVERLAY_*` token family and add `rgba(...)` to the scanner deny list** — with quoted 3/4-digit shorthands now guarded, `rgba(...)` alpha overlays are the LAST color-literal backdoor. An `OVERLAY_DARK_55 = "rgba(38, 27, 20, 0.55)"`-style family (or a `withAlpha(HERO_DARK, 0.55)` helper) plus an `INLINE_RGBA_PATTERN` row in the scanner would close it — the deferred half of the just-shipped task.

- **Add a `PURE_BLACK` token for future true-black needs** — search-overlay's `#000` shadow was routed through `HERO_DARK` per the SHADOW_SOFT convention (warm-tinted shadows), but a future overlay scrim or barcode/QR rendering will legitimately need `#000000`. Pre-registering `PURE_BLACK` next to `PURE_WHITE` keeps the next contributor from reaching for a literal that the scanner now rejects.

- **Fold the shadow style in `search-overlay.tsx` into a `SHADOW_SHEET` token** — the sheet uses a stronger elevation (opacity 0.25, radius 20, elevation 12) than `SHADOW_SOFT`; promoting it to `lib/design-tokens.ts` as a second named shadow (same `as const` object shape) would let the currency sheet, premium upsell sheet, and future bottom sheets share one definition.

- **Teach `pure-white-adoption.test.ts` to walk the whole tree instead of a fixed registry** — the registry pins history, but a tree-wide `assert no quoted shorthand outside HEX_ALLOWLIST` sweep (mirroring check-inline-hex.test.ts's baseline case) inside the adoption test would catch NEW files that sneak in a `"#fff"` even if lint:hex is somehow skipped locally. ~10 lines reusing the same walker.

- **Extend `INLINE_HEX_SHORT_PATTERN` to quoted 8-digit `#rrggbbaa` forms** — today an 8-digit literal is caught only via its leading 6 digits by the main pattern (reported value truncated to `#rrggbb`), which makes the report slightly misleading. A `{3,4}|{8}` alternation (still quote-delimited) would report the full literal and document that RN supports alpha-hex.

## LinearGradient JSX pin follow-ups (2026-07-13)

- **Grep-audit for actual `<LinearGradient>` usage and token-ize any inline stops** — the test pin protects the scanner, but if `expo-linear-gradient` is (or becomes) a dependency, a one-off sweep confirming every `colors={[...]}` array references design tokens would make the pin load-bearing rather than speculative.

- **Pin a template-literal hex case (`` `#${"1"}23456` `` interpolation)** — the scanner catches static template-literal hex via the 6-digit pattern, but interpolated builders slip through by construction (only `lib/placeholder-color.ts` legitimately does this, via allowlist). A test documenting BOTH halves — static backtick hex flags, interpolated does not — would pin the boundary explicitly.

- **Pin a multi-line JSX attribute case** — `colors={[` on one line and `"#001122",` on the next is how Prettier formats long gradient arrays; the line-based scanner catches it (each line is scanned independently), but no test proves it. A 5-line case closes the gap.

- **Extract the repeated `new RegExp(PATTERN.source, "g")` reset into a `freshPattern()` export** — the test file and `findInlineHexLiterals` both re-materialize the module-scope `g`-flag regexes to dodge `lastIndex` state; a tiny exported factory would make the pitfall impossible to reintroduce and shave the duplicated idiom.

- **Add a scanner benchmark guard** — the walk is now 175 files × 2 regexes per line; still instant, but a `console.time` in the script (printed only with `--verbose`) would surface any future pattern addition that accidentally introduces catastrophic backtracking.

## lint:all aggregator follow-ups (2026-07-16)

- **Parallelise the `scripts/lint-all.ts` fan-out** — the guards are independent processes, so running them via `Promise.all` over async `spawn` (bounded to ~4 concurrent) would cut the ~10s aggregate wall-clock to the slowest single guard (~1s); the report already aggregates results, so only the runner loop changes.

- **Ship the discussed guard family members through the new registry** — no-circular-imports (madge-style DFS over relative imports in `lib/`), no-`process.env[varName]` dynamic access (the Metro-inlining foot-gun beyond config resolvers), and no-emoji-in-translation-keys each follow the proven pure-helper + script + registry-entry recipe; each is now a one-entry addition to `LINT_GUARDS` to be CI-enforced.

- **Surface per-guard GitHub annotations from the aggregator** — `lib/check-inline-hex.ts` already has `formatGitHubAnnotations`; lifting the `::error file=…` emission into `lint-all.ts` (each guard's output passed through when `GITHUB_ACTIONS=true`) would give every registry guard click-through PR annotations without per-script wiring.

- **Add a `--only <guard>` / `--skip <guard>` flag to `scripts/lint-all.ts`** — during a migration a contributor often wants the aggregate minus the guard they're mid-way through fixing; a filter over `LINT_GUARDS` plus a report note naming what was skipped keeps the no-silent-caps rule.

- **Emit machine-readable JSON (`--json`) from the aggregator** — `LintGuardResult[]` is already the right shape; a `--json` flag printing it verbatim would let the future dashboard (and the deploy workflow) consume guard status without scraping the human report.

## LazyPhoto follow-ups (2026-07-16)

- **Adopt `LazyPhoto` at the other remote-photo call sites** — `app/item/[id].tsx` (gallery), `app/collection/[id].tsx` (hero cover), `components/collection-card.tsx` and the profile/listing screens still render raw `<Image>` with Cloudinary URLs; each is a mechanical swap now that the component owns skeleton + error fallback.

## getCurrencySymbol follow-ups (2026-07-16)

- **Adopt `getCurrencySymbol` in `<CurrencySheet>` rows** — the full-picker list renders raw codes + names from `lib/currencies.ts`; a leading glyph column (`$ USD — US Dollar`) would make scanning the 150-entry list faster and reuses the cached helper at zero extra cost.

- **Locale-aware symbol derivation** — the helper hard-codes the `"en"` base locale, so `USD` always renders `$`; passing the viewer's locale (via `getDefaultLocaleForLanguage(language)`) would render `US$` in ru/be contexts where the bare `$` is ambiguous with local dollar-priced goods. Needs a decision on cache keying (`locale:code`).

- **Use the glyph in `<CostBadge>` raw mode** — the collection total summary renders `1,500 USD`; an opt-in `symbolDisplay` prop rendering `$1,500` (symbol-position aware via `formatToParts` order) would tighten the summary card. Item mode should keep the code (approx `≈` marker + code is the established pattern).

- **Surface `getCurrencySymbol` in the chip strip's active chip only** — chips stay codes for scannability, but the ACTIVE chip could render `₽ RUB` (glyph + code) to reinforce the mapping the user just picked; one-line change in the `chipCodes.map` render with the existing active branch.

- **Pin a Hermes-parity smoke test in CI** — the helper's `formatToParts`/`narrowSymbol` guards target older Hermes Intl builds, but no automated check runs the helper under an actual Hermes runtime; an `eas build`-time sanity script (or a `react-native` jest preset later) asserting `getCurrencySymbol("USD") !== ""` would catch a Hermes ICU regression before a store release.

## Inline error pills adoption (3b) follow-ups (2026-07-16)

- **Migrate `app/create.tsx`'s cost row onto `<CurrencyInput>`** — the create form still hand-rolls a MaskedTextInput + currency-selector pressable and now renders its own `<ErrorPill>` under the row; swapping to the shared `<CurrencyInput error={...}>` would delete the bespoke costRow styles, pick up the chip strip + glyph symbol for free, and collapse the create/edit cost UX into one component.

- **Focus the invalid field when the pill appears** — a blocked save on create/edit currently shows the pill but leaves scroll position and focus wherever the user was; a `ref` on the cost input + `scrollTo`/`focus()` on the error branch would take users straight to the fix, especially on long forms where the cost row is off-screen.

- **Track a `cost_invalid` analytics event from the create/edit cost fields** — the sell sheet fires `listing_price_invalid` on every blocked submit, but the two cost fields now block saves with zero funnel visibility; reusing the same `{ reason, language }` prop shape (vocabulary already shared via `CurrencyValueError`) would show whether comma-decimal locales trip the cost fields at the same rate as the price field.

- **Type `t()`'s key for `CURRENCY_ERROR_I18N_KEY` values** — the map is `satisfies Record<CurrencyValueError, string>`, so the values are plain strings and a typo'd key would only fail at the test layer; tightening the values to `keyof typeof en` (exported from `lib/i18n-context.tsx`) would make the compiler reject an unknown i18n key at the map definition.

- **Announce pill appearance to screen readers on iOS** — `accessibilityRole="alert"` covers Android/web live-region semantics, but RN on iOS needs an explicit `AccessibilityInfo.announceForAccessibility(label)` when the pill mounts; a `useEffect` inside `ErrorPill` (guarded on `label`) would close the parity gap for VoiceOver users.

## Pinned currencies (MRU chip strip) follow-ups (2026-07-16)

- **Show a subtle "recent" divider between pinned and static chips** — the strip now renders MRU pins ahead of `CURRENCY_CHIPS` with no visual separation; a hairline divider (or slightly different chip border tint) between the two groups would explain WHY the order shifts between form opens, at the cost of ~6 lines in the chip map.

- **Hydrate the pinned list app-wide once instead of per-mount** — every `<CurrencyInput>` mount fires its own `AsyncStorage.getItem`; a tiny module-level cache (invalidate on `pinCurrency`) or a context provider would cut the redundant reads and make the strip order consistent across two simultaneously mounted forms.

- **Pin from the collection display-currency sheet too (behind a prop)** — `app/collection/[id].tsx`'s `<CurrencySheet>` picks deliberately don't pin (display override ≠ money entry), but a `pinOnSelect` prop on the sheet would let a future settings screen opt in without each caller re-rolling the write.

- **Surface the MRU list in a future settings screen** — `getPinnedCurrencies` + `MAX_PINNED_CURRENCIES` give a settings row ("Your currencies: JPY, USD, EUR, GBP — tap to reorder") everything it needs; manual reorder would turn the implicit MRU into explicit user-controlled pinning, matching the original task's "pin the 4 they care about" wording.

- **Migrate the storage pair onto a versioned JSON schema helper** — `parsePinnedCurrencies` hand-rolls JSON-parse + validate + cap; the same shape (parse-or-default with per-element validation) recurs in `marketplace-context` and `collections-context` storage reads. A shared `readJsonArray(key, validateEntry, cap)` helper in `lib/storage-keys.ts` would collapse three hand-rolled readers and pin the never-throws contract in one place.

## CurrencyCode typing follow-ups (2026-07-16)

- **Adopt `CurrencyCode` in the raw-selector call sites** — `app/create.tsx`'s `setCurrency(next: string)` and `app/item/[id].tsx`'s `setListingCurrency`/`setEditCurrency` wrappers still type their params as bare `string`; switching them to the exported `CurrencyCode` propagates the IntelliSense union one hop up at zero runtime cost.

- **Type `CurrencySheet`'s `selectedCode`/`onSelect` with `CurrencyCode`** — the sheet is the component that actually hands back arbitrary ISO codes, so its props are the natural second adoption point for the alias (and would document that the sheet may emit non-shortlist codes).

- **Move `CurrencyCode` next to `CurrencyChipCode` in `lib/locale-helpers.ts`** — the alias currently lives in a component file; co-locating it with the union it extends would let non-component modules (analytics helpers, future settings screens) import it without pulling a react-native component module into scope.

- **Apply the `(string & {})` pattern to `AppLanguage` props** — `lib/i18n-context.tsx`'s `setLanguage(code: string)` has the same shape (6 known codes + forward-compat for new locales); the same intersection trick would surface `"ru" | "en" | ...` in IntelliSense without blocking a future 7th language rollout.

- **Lint-guard against re-introducing bare `currency: string` props** — a lightweight `lint:currency-props` guard (same harness as `lint:hex`) greping component prop types for `currency\??: string\b` would nudge future pickers onto the shared alias before review.

## sanitizeCurrencyInput extraction follow-ups (2026-07-16)

- **Adopt `sanitizeCurrencyInput` in the create form's raw cost input** — `app/create.tsx`'s cost MaskedTextInput doesn't sanitize keystrokes (it relies on the save-time `replace(",", ".")`), so "12,5,0" survives into state; wiring `onChangeText={(v) => setCost(sanitizeCurrencyInput(v))}` gives it the same live normalisation `<CurrencyInput>` users get.

- **Handle locale thousands separators explicitly** — `sanitizeCurrencyInput("1,000.50")` yields `1.00050` because the first comma is treated as THE decimal; a smarter pass (comma is a separator when ≥3 digits follow before another separator) would fix pasted en-US amounts. Needs a truth-table decision first — pin current behaviour as known-lossy in the meantime (test already documents it).

- **Reject a lone "." from reaching state** — the sanitizer can emit `"."` (typed dot first), which `parseCurrencyValueDetailed` classifies as `unparseable` on submit; mapping `"."` → `""` in the sanitizer would keep the empty-optional path (no pill) for a user who fat-fingered a single dot.

- **Share a `MAX_DECIMAL_DIGITS` clamp** — neither the sanitizer nor the parsers cap decimals, so "12.50000001" persists as typed; a `sanitizeCurrencyInput(raw, { maxDecimals: 2 })` option (default off for crypto-friendly flexibility) would pair with the currency-aware minor-units data already in `lib/currencies.ts`.

## EmptyState wrapper audit follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **Promote the audit into a `lint:empty-state` CI script** — `lib/check-empty-state-wrappers.ts` is already a pure matcher in the `lint:hex`/`lint:radius` shape; a 40-line `scripts/check-empty-state-wrappers.ts` walking `app/`+`components/` (copy the `check-inline-radius` CLI) plus a `lint:ci` hook would surface violations as PR annotations instead of only failing `npm test`.

- **Extend the wrapper scan to `<Pressable>` containers** — today `openViewStylesAt` tracks only `<View>` nesting; a caller wrapping `<EmptyState>` in a colored `<Pressable>` (e.g. a whole-card tap target) would slip past. Generalizing the tag regex to `<(View|Pressable)\b` is a two-line change with the same test recipe.

- **Resolve `theme.card` wrapper backgrounds against the active theme** — the allowlist is token-name based (`PAGE_BG_*`, `theme.background`), so a wrapper using `theme.card` correctly flags — but the message can't say WHICH color collides. Piping `lib/design-tokens.ts` values into the report (`CARD_BG_3 = #f3e3cf`) would make the cream-on-cream collision visible in the failure text.

- **Generalize the scanner into a reusable `findWrapperOverrides(componentName, ...)`** — `<SkeletonItemDetail>`, `<CostBadge>` and the future `<IconBadge>` all own token-driven backgrounds that a colored wrapper could mute; parameterizing the target-component regex would let one helper guard the whole family with per-component allowlists.

- **Pin the `theme.card` self-background of EmptyState structurally** — the audit guards callers, but nothing pins that `empty-state.tsx` itself keeps `backgroundColor: theme.card` on `styles.wrap`'s companion inline style; a one-line `assert.match(src, /\{ backgroundColor: theme\.card \}/)` in `empty-state-wrapper-audit.test.ts` closes the other half of the cream-on-cream vector.

## Parser lib unification follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **Fold the create form's save-time `cost.replace(",", ".")` into the parser** — both `app/create.tsx` and the edit form still comma-normalise at the call site before `parseCurrencyValueDetailed`; a `parseCurrencyValueDetailed(raw, { commaTolerant: true })` option (or routing the save-time value through `sanitizeCurrencyInput`) would delete the last two ad-hoc `replace` calls and make the parser the single comma authority.

- **Retire the `classifyInvalidPrice` alias in a call-site sweep** — the classifier is now a one-line delegation; migrating `app/item/[id].tsx`'s `listing_price_invalid` branch onto `parsed.error` directly (the parse result is already in scope) would delete the wrapper, one import, and the delegation pin, leaving `InvalidPriceReason` as a pure type re-export.

- **Move `CURRENCY_ERROR_I18N_KEY` values onto typed i18n keys** — the map's values are plain strings inside a pure lib now; exporting a `TranslationKey = keyof typeof en` from `lib/i18n-context.tsx` and typing the map `satisfies Record<CurrencyValueError, TranslationKey>` would make a typo'd key a compile error instead of a test failure (already logged for the component era — the lib move makes it a 2-line change).

- **Give `parseCurrencyValueDetailed` a `maxDecimals` guard** — the parser accepts "12.500001" for a EUR price; pairing it with the minor-units data in `lib/currencies.ts` (`parseCurrencyValueDetailed(raw, { currency })` → new `too_many_decimals` error) would surface a proper inline pill instead of silently persisting sub-cent amounts, and the vocabulary/i18n plumbing is already in place to grow by one key.

- **Property-test the parser pair with a fuzz sweep** — `parseCurrencyValue` and `parseCurrencyValueDetailed` are pinned to agree on a 12-sample list; a 200-iteration seeded-random string generator (digits/dots/commas/signs/letters mix) asserting `happy === detailed.value && (happy === null) === (detailed.error !== null)` would make the agreement invariant hold for the whole input space, not just the enumerated samples.

## IconBadge extraction follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **Adopt `<IconBadge>` on the premium upsell sheet** — the paywall/upsell surfaces still lead with a bare emoji `<Text>`; swapping in the badge (the extraction's stated first customer) would give the premium prompt the same layered-cream weight as empty states, and the `style` prop already handles the sheet's tighter vertical rhythm.

- **Add a `size` prop that scales all three rings proportionally** — the 96/76/56 step is pinned as intentional, so a `size?: number` (default 96) computing `size`, `size - 20`, `size - 40` (and `fontSize: size * 0.29`) would let compact contexts (list headers, toasts) reuse the visual without breaking the concentric rhythm; the pinned ratio test extends naturally to the derived values.

- **Add a `tone` prop mirroring the proposed EmptyState `tone`** — when EmptyState grows `"neutral" | "premium" | "danger"` variants, the badge is where the gradient actually lives; a matching tone map (`CARD_BG_*` → `MUTED_*` / `DANGER_*` triples) in `icon-badge.tsx` keeps the two props from drifting apart.

- **Promote the ring sizes into `lib/design-tokens.ts` as `RING_OUTER_SIZE` / `RING_MIDDLE_SIZE` / `RING_INNER_SIZE`** — already suggested pre-extraction; now that the literals live in exactly one file the token promotion is a 6-line diff and the icon-badge test's hard-coded `[96, 76, 56]` expectation can import the tokens instead.

- **Register `icon-badge.tsx` in the wrapper-override scanner family** — the badge owns token-driven ring backgrounds exactly like EmptyState's card; once `findWrapperOverrides` is generalized (see EmptyState follow-ups), `<IconBadge>` should be its second guarded component so a colored container can't mute the cream gradient.

## EmptyState tone prop follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **Adopt `tone="danger"` on the destructive empty states** — the bulk-delete "all items deleted" aftermath and the profile-deleted screens still render the warm amber treatment; wiring the new prop at those call sites is a per-line change and gives destructive outcomes the DANGER_DEEP_2 signal the prop shipped for.

- **Thread `tone` through to `<IconBadge>`** — the tone swap covers border + action pill but the icon rings stay cream-to-amber in every tone; a matching `tone` prop on IconBadge (mapping ring gradients per tone) would complete the treatment instead of leaving a warm badge inside a danger-red dashed card.

- **Verify TEXT_ON_DARK_5 contrast on the MUTED_4 action pill** — the neutral tone puts light text on a `#bbb0a6` background, which is likely below WCAG AA for 14px bold; either darken the neutral action (`MUTED_24`?) or flip the action text color per tone via the same TONE_STYLES map.

- **Give `tone="premium"` the richer gradient the task text envisioned** — today premium just re-states AMBER_ACCENT on both slots; a subtle `AMBER_SOFT_*` wrap background (or LinearGradient once one exists in the component set) would actually differentiate premium-locked surfaces from the default treatment.

- **Pin a caller sweep that no call site passes an invalid tone string** — `tone` is a closed union so tsc catches typos, but a structural test greping `tone="..."` usages across `app/`+`components/` against the exported union would also catch a future `tone={variable}` misuse where the variable is a plain string.

## RING_* tokens co-location follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **Extend `lint:radius` with the ring-size literals** — `width: 96` / `76` / `56` inside `components/` are now token-backed; adding the trio to `GEOMETRY_RULES` (word-bounded, `width|height` anchored) would keep a future badge copy from re-inlining the sizes the same way the RADIUS/SPACING rules guard theirs.

- **Derive the icon font size from the ring tokens** — `icon: { fontSize: 28 }` in `icon-badge.tsx` is exactly `RING_INNER_SIZE / 2`; expressing it that way keeps the emoji proportional if the rings are ever retuned, and removes the last magic number in the file.

- **Render the concentric step in the `/_dev/tokens` ring section** — the new preview lists the three sizes next to a live `<IconBadge>`, but doesn't visualise the 20px step; three nested outline circles (like the spacing bars) would make an off-rhythm change visually obvious in PR review.

- **Type the ring trio as a `RingSizes` tuple export** — `[RING_OUTER_SIZE, RING_MIDDLE_SIZE, RING_INNER_SIZE] as const` would give the icon-badge test (and the future `size`-prop scaling math) a single import instead of three, and pins the descending order structurally.

- **Assert the 20px step inside `design-tokens.test.ts` too** — the step is currently pinned in `icon-badge.test.ts`; duplicating the two-line arithmetic check next to the token definitions' own test keeps the invariant visible to someone editing only `lib/design-tokens.ts`.

## Selection-mode getItemLayout follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **Add `getItemLayout` to the viewer FlatList using the same module-level pattern** — the viewer branch renders 2-column masonry rows whose height varies with the compact-card image (110px) + 2-line title, so it needs a per-row-pair height constant (or `numColumns`-aware stride) rather than reusing `SELECTABLE_ROW_HEIGHT`; the `getSelectableRowLayout` shape (module-level, gap-aware offset) is the template.

- **Derive `SELECTABLE_ROW_HEIGHT` arithmetically from the style constants instead of a literal 134** — expressing it as `IMAGE_SIZE + 2 * CARD_PADDING + 2 * CARD_BORDER + 2 * SELECTION_BORDER` (with those four promoted to named consts next to the styles) would make the derivation comment executable, so a future padding tweak can't leave the constant stale.

- **Pin a runtime-parity test that measures the rendered row** — the structural test pins the constant's value but not that it matches reality; once the jsdom/react-test-renderer dev-dep decision lands, a mount-and-measure test (`onLayout` height === `SELECTABLE_ROW_HEIGHT`) would close the drift gap the derivation comment only documents.

- **Handle the tall-text edge case in the row** — `ItemCard`'s text column can exceed the 104px photo column when an item has a long 2-line description *and* a wrapped tags row; either cap the card (`maxHeight` + `overflow: "hidden"` on the selection wrapper) so the fixed-height promise is enforced, or clamp the tags row to one line in selection mode.

- **Reuse the gap-aware stride helper across lists** — `(rowHeight + gap) * index` is about to appear in every `getItemLayout` this codebase grows; a `makeGapAwareItemLayout(rowHeight, gap)` factory in `lib/` (returning the FlatList-shaped function) with its own unit test would keep the offset math single-sourced.

## Handler-stack useCallback follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **Convert `handleMoveTo` to the same hoisted useCallback** — it's the one selection-mode handler left as a plain post-narrow function; it only touches `selectedIds` / `moveItems` / `toast` / `t` / `exitSelectionMode`, so it hoists cleanly and the move-modal rows would then receive a stable callback.

- **Wrap the bulk-bar in a memoized `<BulkBar>` component to actually cash in the stable handlers** — the Pressables live inline in the JSX today, so the useCallback promotion buys referential stability but no skipped renders yet; extracting `<BulkBar count onMove onDelete onCancel>` behind `React.memo` completes the pair.

- **Promote `confirmAndDeleteCollection` / `handleDeleteCollection` into the hoisted stack** — they close over `activeCollection.id`, so hoisting means switching to nullable `collection?.id` with an early bail; doing it would let the header action row memoize the same way the bulk-bar can.

- **Add a lint gate for post-early-return hook declarations** — the Rules-of-Hooks hazard this migration dodged (a `useCallback` accidentally declared after `if (!collection) return`) is exactly what `eslint-plugin-react-hooks`' `rules-of-hooks` catches; wiring it into `npm run lint:ci` (expo lint already ships the plugin) would make the structural test's placement pin redundant-in-depth.

- **Memoize `handleRefresh` too and thread it into `RefreshControl`** — it's re-allocated every render and passed to `<RefreshControl onRefresh>`; a `useCallback` on `[refresh, params.id]` plus the existing stable setters would finish the handler sweep for the pull-to-refresh path.

## selectedById map follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **Benchmark the Set-vs-object claim before propagating the pattern** — the task's premise (hidden-class property read beats `Set.prototype.has`) is engine- and size-dependent; a 20-line `node --test` micro-benchmark over 1k/10k ids would either justify copying the pattern elsewhere or argue for reverting to the simpler Set read.

- **Reuse `selectedById` in the bulk-bar count and guards** — `selectedIds.size` still drives the bulk-bar label and disabled states; if the map ever becomes the single selection representation, deriving `selectedCount = Object.keys(selectedById).length` (or keeping the Set as source of truth and map as derived view, as today) should be an explicit, documented choice.

- **Consider `Set<string>` → `Record<string, true>` as the state itself** — today the state is a Set and the map is derived; storing the object directly would drop the per-toggle `Object.fromEntries` rebuild and make `toggleSelect` a spread-and-delete. Worth weighing against the Set's nicer `size`/iteration ergonomics.

- **Type `selectedById` explicitly as `Record<string, true>`** — `Object.fromEntries` infers `{ [k: string]: boolean }`-ish shapes; an explicit annotation would document the invariant that absent keys (not `false` values) encode "unselected".

- **Extend the selection stability-chain comment into the docs sweep** — the `selectedIds → selectedById → renderSelectableRow` chain now spans three declarations; a short block comment above the selection-state `useState` naming the chain would spare the next reader the archaeology.

## Selection Profiler follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **Add a matching Profiler around the viewer FlatList** — the viewer branch is the higher-traffic path (every visitor hits it; selection mode is owner-only); the same `onRender` callback with a second id (`"viewer-flatlist"`) would cover both memoization surfaces for one more prop.

- **Aggregate instead of streaming** — per-commit `console.log` lines get noisy during a rapid multi-select; accumulating `{commits, totalMs, maxMs}` in a ref and logging a summary when selection mode exits would keep Metro readable while preserving the signal.

- **Warn, don't log, above a threshold** — a `actualDuration > 16` (one frame) branch that uses `console.warn` would make regressions stand out in Metro's default filter instead of scrolling past as info noise.

- **Drop the `selectedIds` dep via a ref** — the callback currently rebuilds every toggle because it closes over `selectedIds`; reading a `selectedCountRef` (kept in sync by `toggleSelect`) would let the callback have `[]` deps, at the cost of the ref-sync plumbing. Marginal; only worth it if the Profiler wrapper ever moves to a memoized subtree.

- **Pin a prod-strip verification once a bundle-analysis test exists** — the `__DEV__` gate should dead-code-eliminate the log body from the export bundle; the existing `bundle-size.test.ts` harness could grep the emitted dist JS for `"[profiler]"` to prove the string never ships.

## Named-memo pin follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **Apply the named-function pin to `ItemCard` too** — `components/item-card.tsx` uses the same `memo(function ItemCard ...)` form; a parallel assertion in `collection-detail-itemcard-memo.test.ts` would close the same DevTools-anonymity gap for the higher-traffic card component.

- **Generalize into a repo-wide memo-naming sweep test** — a single test that greps `components/*.tsx` for `memo\(\s*\(` (anonymous-arrow memo) and fails with the offending file list would enforce the named form everywhere without per-component pins.

- **Pin the `displayName` alternative as acceptable** — `memo(fn); Component.displayName = "X"` also names the component in DevTools; if the sweep test lands, accept either form so future components aren't forced into one style.

- **Audit hooks for the same anonymity issue** — `useCallback`-produced render functions (e.g. `renderSelectableRow`) show as anonymous in profiler flamegraphs too; naming the inner arrows (`useCallback(function renderSelectableRow(...))`) is the same one-line fix if profiling sessions get confusing.

- **Dedup sweep for the remaining VM-E section** — two more VM-E bullets (bulk-bar hoist, onEndReached pagination) partially overlap later VM-D bullets; a one-pass reconciliation of the VM-D/E sections in `.tasks.md` would prevent future runs re-reading superseded context.

## BulkBar extraction follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **Reuse `<BulkBar>` in the wishlist screen when it grows bulk actions** — the component's props are generic (`count` + three callbacks); if wishlist ever ships multi-select, the bar drops in as-is instead of a re-extraction.

- **Add a `busy` prop for in-flight bulk operations** — `performBulkDelete`/`handleMoveTo` are async but the bar stays fully interactive while they run; a `busy` boolean that disables all three buttons (and swaps the count for a spinner) would prevent double-submits on slow connections.

- **Animate the bar in/out** — the bar pops in instantly when selection mode toggles; a `react-native-reanimated` slide-up (already a transitive dep via draggable-flatlist) or a plain `Animated.timing` translate would soften the transition without new packages.

- **Move the spacer into the component as a static `BulkBar.SPACER_HEIGHT` export** — `styles.bulkBarSpacer { height: 120 }` in the page silently encodes the bar's height; exporting the constant from `bulk-bar.tsx` (and using it in the spacer) keeps the two in lockstep like `SELECTABLE_ROW_HEIGHT` does for rows.

- **Safe-area-inset the bar's bottom padding** — `paddingBottom: 18` ignores the home-indicator inset on modern iPhones; `useSafeAreaInsets().bottom` (already available via expo-router's provider) folded into the padding would keep the cancel button clear of the gesture bar.

## BB-B scroll-ownership follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **BB-C is now unblocked** — with the selection FlatList owning scroll, re-tune `windowSize`/`initialNumToRender` for a scrolling list (the nested-list values were chosen when the outer ScrollView drove visibility) and consider `onEndReached` to replace the Load-more CTA in this branch.

- **Add a RefreshControl to the selection branch** — the old nestable Screen provided pull-to-refresh in selection mode; the new early return doesn't. Decide whether refresh-during-selection is wanted (it may clear `selectedIds` when items reload) and either wire the viewer branch's RefreshControl or document the intentional omission.

- **Preserve scroll position across mode transitions** — entering/exiting selection mode now swaps between two different scroll owners (outer ScrollView vs FlatList), so the user's scroll offset resets. Capturing the offset at toggle time and `scrollToOffset`-ing after the swap would smooth the transition.

- **Reuse the selection branch's header-measurement for the viewer branch** — the viewer FlatList's future `getItemLayout` (still an open suggestion) will hit the same ListHeaderComponent offset problem; `selectionHeaderHeight`/`onSelectionHeaderLayout` generalize to a `useMeasuredHeader()` hook both branches can share.

- **Empty-selection edge: exiting via 0-item delete** — `performBulkDelete` can delete every item, making `allItems.length > 0` false while `selectionMode` is still true for a frame; verify the fallback return renders the empty state cleanly in that frame (it appears to — `exitSelectionMode()` runs after the await — but a regression test pinning the exit call order would lock it).

## BB-C windowing follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **Profile the 7-viewport window with the shipped dev Profiler** — the `[profiler] selection-flatlist` Metro logs now measure exactly what BB-C tuned; a quick dev session scrolling a 200-item collection would confirm 7 beats 5 (or argue for 9) with data instead of heuristics.

- **Consider `maintainVisibleContentPosition` for prepend-safe scrolling** — if items ever insert at the top of the selection list (e.g. a future sort-while-selecting), the scroll-owning FlatList can pin the viewport with one prop; the nested shape never could.

- **Align the two branches' tuning into a shared constant object** — `initialNumToRender`/`maxToRenderPerBatch`/`removeClippedSubviews` are duplicated between viewer and selection FlatLists with only `windowSize` differing; a `LIST_TUNING = { ... }` spread (with per-branch windowSize) would keep future tweaks single-sourced.

- **Try `getItemLayout`-driven `initialScrollIndex` for selection re-entry** — with row geometry known, re-entering selection mode could restore the last scroll position via `initialScrollIndex` instead of starting at the top.

- **Measure `removeClippedSubviews` on Android** — it's currently iOS-only (matching VM-D), but a scroll-owning selection list may benefit on low-end Android too; RN's Android clipping bugs have narrowed since the prop was gated.

## onEndReached pagination follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **Debounce/guard rapid onEndReached bursts with an `onMomentumScrollBegin` latch** — RN's FlatList can fire `onEndReached` more than once per approach on some platforms (especially web); today each extra call is a cheap clamped state set, but the classic `momentumLatch.current` pattern would make the window grow exactly one page per user gesture.

- **Show a lightweight "loading more" footer spinner during window extension** — the CTA's removal means there's no visual feedback when the next page mounts; a tiny `ActivityIndicator` footer shown while `hasMore` (mirroring marketplace/chat list patterns) would signal that more content is coming without restoring the button.

- **Drop the `loadMoreItems*` i18n keys once the drag branch migrates off the manual CTA** — the three keys (× 6 languages) now serve only the nestable drag-mode fallback; if drag-mode ever gets its own scroll-owning list (the queued draggable-flatlist migration), the CTA and its keys can be deleted wholesale.

- **Tune `onEndReachedThreshold` per branch density** — the viewer's 2-column masonry consumes rows twice as fast per viewport as the single-column selection list; a slightly higher threshold (e.g. 0.8) on the viewer branch would start the next page earlier on fast flicks.

- **Consider promoting `useChunkedList` + onEndReached into a `usePaginatedFlatListProps()` helper** — the `{ data: visibleItems, onEndReached: hasMore ? loadMore : undefined, onEndReachedThreshold: 0.5 }` triple will repeat verbatim when `app/wishlist.tsx` / `app/collections-feed.tsx` pick up the queued FlatList migration; one hook returning spreadable props closes the duplication class before the second consumer lands.

## Viewer getItemLayout follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **Extract the measured-header pattern into a `useMeasuredHeight()` hook** — `selectionHeaderHeight`/`onSelectionHeaderLayout` and the new `viewerHeaderHeight`/`onViewerHeaderLayout` are byte-identical 4-line pairs; a `const [height, onLayout] = useMeasuredHeight()` hook in `lib/` would collapse both and serve every future ListHeaderComponent-offset consumer (wishlist/collections-feed FlatList migrations).

- **Derive `COMPACT_ITEM_CARD_HEIGHT` from the style object at module scope instead of a hand-summed literal** — `styles.compactImage.height + 2 * SPACING_INLINE + styles.compactTitle.minHeight + ...` computed from the actual `StyleSheet.create` input would make the constant impossible to drift from the styles; the test pinning 190 stays as the human-readable checksum.

- **Reserve the 2-line title block only when `compact` grid mode needs it** — the fixed `minHeight: 36` makes every 1-line-title card 18px taller than before; if design ever wants tighter cards back, a `fixedHeight?: boolean` prop on `ItemCard` (true only from the viewer FlatList) would restore the compact look elsewhere while keeping the grid deterministic.

- **Unify the selection + viewer getItemLayout row-stride math into one helper** — `header + gap + (row + gap) * rowIndex` appears twice with different constants; a pure `listRowOffset({ headerHeight, gap, rowHeight, rowIndex })` in `lib/` with unit tests would make a third consumer (future wishlist FlatList) a one-liner and let the two screens share regression coverage.

- **Feed `initialScrollIndex` from the last-viewed item on viewer re-entry** — with row geometry now known up-front, returning to a collection from an item detail could restore the scroll position via `initialScrollIndex={Math.floor(lastIndex / 2)}` with zero measurement cost — the exact capability getItemLayout unlocks.

## data={visibleItems} guard follow-ups (2026-07-17)

- **Generalize the unbounded-data guard into a repo-wide lint rule** — the blanket `doesNotMatch(data={items|allItems|filteredItems})` protects one file; a `lint:*` guard (same pure-helper + CLI trio as lint:radius) scanning every `app/**` screen for `data={<unwindowed array>}` next to a `useChunkedList` import would protect the queued wishlist/collections-feed FlatList migrations for free.

- **Pin the reverse direction: every FlatList in collection/[id].tsx must reference visibleItems** — the blanket negative catches the known-bad names but a renamed unbounded array (`sortedItems`) would slip through; asserting each `<FlatList` block contains `data={visibleItems}` closes the rename loophole.

- **Extend the guard to `extraData`** — `extraData={items}` on a memoized list would force full re-renders on every items change even with windowed data; cheap companion assertion while the regex machinery is in place.

- **Turn the three positive-pin test files' shared `readSrc()` into a fixture helper** — five collection-detail test files each re-declare `readFileSync(path.join(...))`; a `__tests__/helpers/read-source.ts` would shrink each and standardize path handling for future screen tests.

- **Add a chunked-window invariants test at the hook level** — `useChunkedList` guarantees `visibleItems.length <= items.length` and `<= ceil(count/pageSize)*pageSize`; a property-style sweep over random lengths would pin the clamp math against future pageSize tuning.

## HM-A header-fragment memoization follow-ups (2026-07-18)

- **Ship HM-B's handler promotion as its own reviewable commit before touching pageHeader** — `openEditModal` / `handleExportPdf` / `handleDeleteCollection` becoming nullable-`collection` useCallbacks is behavior-preserving on its own and shrinks the risky HM-B diff to just the `useMemo` wrap; the established `collection-detail-handler-usecallback.test.ts` registry can absorb the three new cases with zero new files.

- **Give `useChunkedList` a `loadMoreRef`-style stable identity across `items` swaps** — `loadMore` is now stable per items-identity, but an items swap still re-creates it (harmless today because the CTA memo re-fires anyway). Routing `items.length` through a ref (`const totalRef = useRef(items.length); totalRef.current = items.length`) would make `loadMore` empty-dep stable forever, letting future consumers pass it to deeply-memoized children without any dep churn.

- **Memoize the ListHeaderComponent WRAPPER elements too** — both scroll-owning branches still allocate a fresh `<View onLayout={...}>{pageHeader}...</View>` element per render, so React reconciles the wrapper (cheap) before bailing on the memoized children. Once HM-B lands, a `selectionListHeader = useMemo(...)` / `viewerListHeader = useMemo(...)` pair keyed on the fragment identities + the onLayout callbacks would make the entire header subtree bail out in one comparison.

- **Pin a repo-wide "no fresh-arrow returns from custom hooks" lint sweep** — the `loadMore` instability HM-A fixed is a class, not an instance: any custom hook returning inline arrows (`lib/` hooks that expose handlers) silently defeats consumer memos. A structural test grepping `lib/use-*.ts` for `const \w+ = \(\) =>` outside `useCallback` would catch the next one at authoring time.

- **Measure the HM-A win with the existing VM-F Profiler before investing in HM-C** — the `__DEV__` Profiler wrapper already logs per-commit `actualDuration` for the selection FlatList; comparing scroll-driven commit times before/after HM-A (and after HM-B) would give the HM-C evaluate-first task the numbers it's supposed to decide on, instead of shipping a dozen-dep `modalsBlock` memo on faith.

## HM-B pageHeader memoization follow-ups (2026-07-18)

- **Narrow the `theme` dep to the four fields the summary cards read** — the pageHeader memo re-fires whenever `useAppTheme()` returns a new object even if `card`/`border`/`text`/`meta` are unchanged; destructuring those four into locals above the memo (and listing them as deps) would keep the header stable across theme-object identity churn.

- **Split the total-cost IIFE out of the pageHeader factory into its own memoized `<TotalCostCard>`** — `getCollectionTotalCost` walks every item's cost on each factory run; a child component keyed on `[collection.id, collection.currency, allItems]` would contain the recompute AND drop the `getCollectionTotalCost` dep from the header memo entirely.

- **Promote `handleRefresh` and `handleMoveTo` to the useCallback stack too** — they're the last plain-function handlers below the early returns; promoting them (nullable-guarded like the HM-B four) would let the drag-mode branch's `<Screen nestable onRefresh>` and the move-modal rows see stable references, and clears the path for HM-C's modal-extraction option (b).

- **Pin a structural test that the pageHeader factory contains NO inline `useState` setter closures beyond the currency-picker's** — the `openCurrencyPicker` arrow inside the factory is deliberate (it closes over `total`), but a future edit adding more inline arrows would silently grow the re-render surface; a count assertion on `=> {` occurrences inside the extracted factory block would flag the drift.

- **Measure the before/after with the VM-F Profiler and record the numbers in the HM-C evaluation** — HM-B's win (hero Image element stability) is the parent task's headline claim; logging scroll-commit `actualDuration` deltas on a 200-item collection would turn the HM-C decide-against-measurements task from a promise into a one-session check.

## HM-C1 MoveCollectionModal extraction follow-ups (2026-07-18)

- **Fold the duplicated `modalBackdrop`/`modalTitle`/`modalCancel`/`modalCancelText` styles into a shared `components/modal-chrome.ts`** — HM-C1 deliberately duplicated the four shared modal styles (the page's edit modal still consumes them until HM-C3); once HM-C3 lands, a tiny shared style module (or a `<ModalCard>` wrapper component owning backdrop + card + title + cancel slots) would keep the three extracted modals visually lock-stepped instead of drifting one `padding` tweak at a time.

- **Promote the `rgba(26, 14, 6, 0.55)` backdrop literal into the long-queued `OVERLAY_*` token family** — the move-modal extraction copied the rgba literal verbatim because `lint:hex` only guards hex; the same overlay value now lives in TWO files (page + component), which is exactly the drift class the OVERLAY family was proposed to close. Shipping `OVERLAY_BACKDROP_55` in `lib/design-tokens.ts` + adopting it at both sites would also unblock the rgba-scanner half of the 3-digit-shorthand task.

- **Give `<MoveCollectionModal>` an empty-state row** — today `handleOpenMove` guards the zero-other-collections case with a toast before opening, so the modal can never render an empty list from the selection flow; but the component contract allows `collections={[]}` and would render a title + cancel with a blank middle. A `t("noOtherCollections")` fallback row inside the component would make it safe for future callers that don't pre-guard.

- **Thread `disabled`/`in-flight` state through `onMove`** — `handleMoveTo` closes the modal optimistically before `await moveItems(...)` resolves; a slow AsyncStorage write leaves a gap where the user can re-enter selection mode against stale rows. Returning the promise to the component and rendering a per-row spinner (the `editSaving` pattern) would close the gap without a global busy flag.

- **Pin a `modalsBlock` shrink-tracking assertion** — HM-C's whole arc is shrinking the ~280-line fragment to a composition point; a structural test asserting the `const modalsBlock = (` block spans under N source lines (ratcheted down as C2/C3/C4 land, ending near ~20) would stop inline JSX from creeping back into the block between extractions.

## HM-C2 CollectionShareSheet extraction follow-ups (2026-07-18)

- **Retire the sheet's bare `setTimeout` for the copied-state reset via a cleanup-aware effect** — the extraction preserved the page's original `setTimeout(() => setLinkCopied(false), 2000)` verbatim, but now that the state lives in a component that can UNMOUNT (modal torn down while the timer is pending), the reset fires a state set on an unmounted component. A `useEffect`-scoped timer keyed on `linkCopied` (or the queued `useShareLink` hook) would add the missing cleanup for ~5 LOC.

- **Unify with the queued `<ShareSheet>` / `useShareLink` suggestions from the item-screen backlog** — `app/item/[id].tsx` still carries its own ~150-line inline share sheet with the same handle/title/link-box/copy/native/cancel skeleton; now that the collection variant is a component, the two should converge on one primitive (the collection sheet's friend-sharing section becoming an optional slot/children prop) before a third screen (profile share) lands a fourth copy.

- **Move the `ensureProfilesLoaded(sharedWithUserIds)` prefetch into the sheet itself** — today the page watches `shareOpen` to prefetch viewer profiles, which is the last share-specific logic left outside the component; passing `ensureProfilesLoaded` as a prop and effect-ing on `visible && sharedWithUserIds.length` inside would complete the encapsulation and drop the `shareOpen` dep from the page effect list.

- **Type the `getProfileById` prop against a narrowed `Pick<UserProfile, "avatar" | "displayName" | "username">`** — the sheet only reads three fields but the prop demands the full `UserProfile`, so any future profile-shape change ripples into the component's type surface for fields it never touches; a Pick keeps the contract honest and documents what the sheet actually consumes.

- **Promote the `rgba(38, 27, 20, 0.4)` / `rgba(26, 14, 6, 0.55)` backdrop pair into the OVERLAY_* family in one pass** — the two extractions have now copied BOTH modal-backdrop rgba literals into component files (share sheet + move modal) while the page still carries its own copies for the edit modal; when the OVERLAY tokens land, four files need the swap — do it as one sweep with an adoption test so the backdrops can't drift shade-by-shade.

## HM-C3 EditCollectionModal extraction follow-ups (2026-07-18)

- **Move the edit-form draft state into a `useEditCollectionDraft(collection)` hook** — the page still carries 7 `useState` pairs + `openEditModal`'s seeding block purely for the modal; a hook returning `{ draft, setField, seedFrom, reset }` would collapse ~30 page lines and give HM-C4's currency sheet a single draft object to read `editCurrency` from, without changing the component's prop surface.

- **Fold the three modal-chrome style copies into a shared `<ModalCard>` wrapper now that all consumers are components** — `modalBackdrop`/`modalTitle`/`modalCancel`/`modalCancelText` are duplicated verbatim across `move-collection-modal.tsx` and `edit-collection-modal.tsx` (and the backdrop shape again in the share sheet); with zero page consumers left the shared-chrome component no longer risks a page/component split-brain, so the dedup is purely mechanical.

- **Give the save button a pending-state a11y announcement** — `saving` flips the label to `t("saving")` and disables the Pressable, but there's no `accessibilityState={{ disabled: saving, busy: saving }}`, so VoiceOver users get no feedback that the tap registered; a 2-line prop addition on `editSaveButton` closes it.

- **Debounce or single-flight `handleSaveEdit`** — the hoisted callback guards on `editSaving` only via the button's `disabled`, but `onRequestClose`+rapid double-tap on web can still race two saves (each awaiting `uploadImage`); an early `if (editSaving) return;` inside the callback (it already deps on nothing mutable mid-flight) would make the guard state-level instead of UI-level.

- **Pin a props-parity test between `openEditModal`'s seeds and the component's value props** — `openEditModal` seeds exactly the 6 fields the modal renders (+`editCoverChanged`); a structural test asserting every `setEditX(collection...)` seed has a matching `x={editX}` prop on the `<EditCollectionModal>` call site would catch a future field added to one side but not the other (the classic stale-draft bug).

## HM-C4 CurrencySheet memoization follow-ups (2026-07-18)

- **Stabilize the OTHER CurrencySheet call sites now that the component is memoized** — `app/create.tsx`, `app/settings.tsx`, and `components/currency-input.tsx` still pass inline `onSelect`/`onClose` arrows, so the new memo never skips there; each is the same two-useCallback promotion the collection page got, and the create screen re-renders on every keystroke of the item form (the biggest remaining win).

- **Virtualize the CurrencySheet list with FlatList** — the sheet renders all ~160 ISO currencies through a plain ScrollView `.map`; the VM-series FlatList recipe (fixed row height → getItemLayout) applies directly and would cut the sheet's open-animation jank on low-end devices.

- **Extract the filter into `lib/currency-filter.ts`** — the `query.trim().toLowerCase()` + code/name `includes` matcher inside the component's useMemo is node-pure and would get a real unit-test matrix (diacritics, symbol search like "$", empty query) once extracted; today it's only exercised structurally.

- **Add `accessibilityState={{ selected }}` to each currency row** — rows mark selection with a checkmark icon only, so VoiceOver reads all rows identically; the same 1-line fix already queued for the sort chips applies here.

- **Pin a "memoized components receive no inline arrows" sweep test for the whole modalsBlock** — HM-C4's no-`=>` guard covers the CurrencySheet call site; generalizing it to assert NONE of the four modalsBlock children carry an inline-arrow prop would keep the whole HM-C invariant from regressing one prop at a time.

## Responsive masonry columnCount follow-ups (2026-07-18)

- **Widen `contentMaxWidth` on the collection viewer branch when 3-4 columns are active** — `useResponsive().contentMaxWidth` caps desktop content at 860px, so 4 columns each get ~200px and the compact cards' 110px images sit in noticeably narrow cells; either bump the cap for grid screens (a `wide` prop on `<Screen>`) or derive the count from the actual content width (`Math.floor(contentWidth / MIN_CARD_WIDTH)`) instead of breakpoint flags.

- **Reset or clamp the chunked-list window on a column-count remount** — the `key` remount throws away FlatList scroll position but `useChunkedList`'s window survives, so after a desktop→mobile resize the list re-mounts at the top with potentially hundreds of already-paged-in rows mounted; a `useEffect` on `masonryColumnCount` that shrinks the window back to the first chunk would restore the memory bound the VM series shipped.

- **Thread `masonryColumnCount` into the `windowSize` heuristic** — the viewer FlatList keeps `windowSize={5}` with a comment reasoning about "2-column masonry mounts twice the cards per viewport"; at 4 columns a viewport mounts twice as many cards again, so `windowSize` could drop to 4 (or the comment should be updated to acknowledge the responsive count) to keep the memory envelope flat across breakpoints.

- **Share the breakpoint→count mapping with the collections-feed and profile grids when they gain multi-column layouts** — the `isDesktop ? 4 : isTablet ? 3 : 2` ternary is inline in `app/collection/[id].tsx`; a `useMasonryColumnCount()` hook next to `useResponsive()` in `components/screen.tsx` would keep future grid screens from drifting to different breakpoint counts.

- **Handle the `getItemLayout` header-height race on remount** — `viewerHeaderHeight` state survives the column-count remount but the new list re-measures the header via `onLayout` one frame later; if a resize also reflows the header (it does — text wraps differently at new widths), the first-frame offsets are computed from the stale height. Deriving `getItemLayout` only once `viewerHeaderHeight > 0` for the *current* key (e.g. resetting the measured height on count change) would close the one-frame mis-scroll window.

## Masonry modulo-guard follow-ups (2026-07-18)

- **Promote the modulo-by-2 guard into a repo-wide sweep test (`masonry-callers.test.ts`)** — the new pin covers `app/collection/[id].tsx` only; the queued VM-B bullet about asserting every future grid caller uses FlatList/`distributeIntoMasonryColumns` instead of an inline `i % N` split is now a 10-line `grep`-walk over `app/**` + `components/**` mirroring the design-tokens hex-gate shape.

- **Decide `lib/masonry.ts`'s fate now that no screen imports it** — the helper survived VM-C "for future reuse" but has had zero consumers for a while; either delete it (its tests too — the FlatList recipe replaced the need) or keep it explicitly for the SwipeTabs-constrained collections-feed grid where a scroll-owning FlatList can't mount.

- **Generalize the identifier-mod-N guard into a shared test util** — `doesNotMatch(/[A-Za-z_$][\w$]*\s*%\s*2\b/)` is the third "absence of a re-rolled shape" regex in the collection-detail suite (after the no-inline-setTimeout and no-hand-rolled-hasPhoto guards); a `assertNoReRoll(src, pattern, hint)` helper in a `__tests__/helpers/` module would make the failure messages uniform and searchable.

- **Pin the selection-branch's single-column assumption** — `getSelectableRowLayout` multiplies by bare `index` (implicit 1-column); if the selection list ever gains `numColumns` the offsets silently corrupt exactly like the viewer divisor would have. A one-line `doesNotMatch(/<FlatList[^>]*renderItem=\{renderSelectableRow\}[\s\S]*?numColumns/)`-style guard closes the mirror-image hole.

- **Audit other screens for hard-coded 2-column math while they're still single-shape** — `app/collections-feed.tsx` and profile grids render single-column today, but when they adopt the multi-column recipe the same three-leg agreement (numColumns / getItemLayout divisor / remount key) applies; documenting the recipe once in `lib/flat-list-styles.ts`'s doc comment would make the next migration copy the safe shape instead of the literal.

## distributeByHeight follow-ups (2026-07-18)

- **Adopt `distributeByHeight` the day compact cards go variable-height** — the tie-break parity with round-robin means the swap is behaviour-neutral until heights actually diverge; the natural trigger is the queued "compact cards carry price/condition extras" work, and the adoption point would be a manual-column layout (FlatList `numColumns` can't do balanced masonry — it's row-locked).

- **Note the FlatList/numColumns vs balanced-masonry tension in `lib/masonry.ts`'s doc block** — `numColumns` produces row-aligned grids, so TRUE balanced masonry needs the manual-column shape the VM series just removed; documenting that trade-off (row-locked + virtualized vs balanced + unwindowed, or a library like @shopify/flash-list masonry mode) prevents a future contributor from "upgrading" the viewer to distributeByHeight and silently losing virtualization.

- **Consider a `getHeight` memo contract** — distributeByHeight calls `getHeight` once per item per distribution; if a caller derives height from image aspect ratios (async), a `Map<itemId, number>` cache parameter (or documenting that getHeight must be O(1)) keeps a 4-column desktop re-layout cheap.

- **Property-test the two distributors' shared invariants** — total item count preserved, per-column order preserved, every item in exactly one column; a 20-line fuzz loop (seeded PRNG, no dev-dep needed) over random sizes/counts would cover both helpers' contracts far past the hand-written matrix.

- **Use a min-heap if column counts ever exceed the single digits** — the shortest-column scan is O(columns) per item (fine at 2-4); the doc comment could pin that assumption so a future "grid of 12 thumbnails" consumer knows to revisit.

## useMasonryColumns / masonry-callers sweep follow-ups (2026-07-18)

- **Fold the masonry-callers walker into a shared `__tests__` walk helper** — `masonry-callers.test.ts`, `radius-pill-adoption.test.ts`-style registries, and the lint-guard CLIs each carry their own recursive `.ts(x)` walker; one `walkSourceFiles(roots)` util (or promoting the sweep into the `GEOMETRY_RULES`-style lint family) would kill the copies and give every future sweep the same self-guard shape.

- **Promote the modulo sweep from test to lint guard** — the `LINT_GUARDS` registry pattern (pure matcher + CLI + registry row) would surface offenders as CI annotations with file:line instead of a bare assert.deepEqual diff; the matcher is already written, the move is mechanical.

- **Give `useMasonryColumns` its first consumer deliberately** — the natural candidate is a profile-screen "pinned items" grid or the future balanced-height masonry; when it lands, add the adoption pin BEFORE wiring so the dep-array contract (`[items, columnCount]`, narrowed window only) is enforced from day one.

- **Consider a `useMasonryColumnsByHeight` sibling when distributeByHeight gains a consumer** — same memo shape, `getHeight` kept in a ref (the `useDwellTimeEffect` latest-ref idiom) so an inline `(item) => item.h` closure doesn't re-fire the memo every render.

- **Sweep `lib/` too once a legitimate mod-2 consumer appears** — the sweep deliberately covers `app/` + `components/` only (lib/masonry.ts itself mods by column count); if UI-adjacent helpers ever move into `lib/`, widening the roots + allowlisting `masonry.ts` mirrors the `HEX_ALLOWLIST` shape.

### TAG_COLORS rotation + nextTagColor (2026-08-07)

- **Reclaim a freed hue when a tag is deleted, not just when one is added** — `nextTagColor(usedColors)` fixes the collision at ADD time, but a collection that has cycled through 15+ tags still ends up with two visible tags sharing a colour once all 10 slots are taken and the fallback rotation kicks in. A `rebalanceTagColors(tags)` pass (stable-sort by current index, reassign greedily) run on save would keep the whole set distinct instead of just the newest member. Cheap to write on top of the existing pure helper and node-testable.

- **Persist the tag colour by label, not by position** — today the same label ("sealed") gets a different hue in every item, because the rotation only ever looks at the tags on the item in front of it. Hashing the lowercased label into `TAG_COLORS` (the `lib/placeholder-color.ts` deterministic-palette idiom, already in the repo) would make a tag read the same colour everywhere it appears, which is the whole point of colour-coding. Migration-free: existing `tag.color` values stay authoritative, only newly-added tags derive.

- **Extract the trim → case-insensitive-dedupe → append handler into `useTagInput(tags, setTags)`** — `app/create.tsx` and `app/item/[id].tsx` now each have a hand-written `addTag()` with identical semantics (only the state-variable names differ). A ~25 LOC hook returning `{ input, setInput, addTag, canAdd }` would collapse both and give the third consumer (the listing form's tag field, when it lands) the behaviour for free. Mirrors the `useShareLink` extraction shipped 2026-08-07.

- **Surface a "tag already added" hint instead of silently swallowing the submit** — both screens `return` early when the label duplicates an existing tag, so pressing Add on a duplicate looks like a broken button. A one-line `toast.info(t("tagsDuplicate"))` (or an inline `<ErrorPill>`, already imported in `create.tsx`) closes the dead-input feel for ~5 LOC + 6 locale strings.

- **Promote `lint:hex`'s short-hex half into the per-file adoption sweeps** — `__tests__/tag-colors-rotation.test.ts` runs the real `findInlineHexLiterals` scanner over the two tag screens and asserts zero matches at any width, which is strictly stronger than the `#[0-9a-fA-F]{6}` regex the ~40 per-file design-tokens pins still use. Swapping those pins onto the shared scanner (one helper, ~40 one-line edits) would close the same shorthand blind spot everywhere at once and delete 40 copies of a regex that has already been superseded.

### Reorder-blocked-by-sort notice (2026-08-07)

- **Auto-reset the sort when the owner enters reorder mode, instead of explaining why it's off** — the notice fixes the "broken button" feel, but the owner still has to make two taps to do the thing they asked for. Having `setReorderMode(true)` also clear the sort (with an undo affordance in the same toast) turns a dead end into a direct path. The notice stays as the fallback for the case where the owner re-applies a sort WHILE reorder mode is on.

- **Disable the "Reorder" button while a sort is active rather than letting it activate inertly** — a `disabled` + `accessibilityState={{ disabled: true }}` + `accessibilityHint={t("reorderBlockedBySort")}` treatment on the actions-row button surfaces the constraint BEFORE the tap, which is a shorter loop than surfacing it after. Pairs with (not replaces) the notice: screen-reader users get the reason from the hint without having to discover the alert.

- **Extract the notice into a reusable `<InlineNotice tone icon message actionLabel onAction>`** — the same "explain a blocked state + offer the one-tap fix" shape already exists inline in `<EmptyState>`'s action row and the premium-upsell surfaces, and the wishlist/marketplace screens both have TODO-shaped versions. One ~40 LOC component with `tone: "info" | "warning"` would dedupe three call sites and give the app a consistent inline-alert language.

- **Pin the reorder/sort interaction with a state-machine table test** — the gate now spans four booleans (`isOwner`, `selectionMode`, `reorderMode`, `sort === "default"`), and `isDragBranch` + `reorderBlockedBySort` + `isViewerFlatListBranch` must stay mutually consistent across all 16 combinations. Extracting the three derivations into a pure `deriveCollectionListBranch(flags)` helper in `lib/` would make that a 16-row `assert.deepEqual` table instead of three regexes over the screen source.

- **Persist `reorderMode` per collection so it survives a navigation round-trip** — today the owner enters reorder mode, taps into an item to check something, comes back, and the mode is gone (it's plain `useState`). Keying it off the collection id in the existing `collectables-*` AsyncStorage namespace (or just a screen-level ref keyed by `params.id`) is a few lines and removes a small repeated annoyance during a long reordering session.

### SORT_OPTIONS + sort chip a11y (2026-08-07)

- **Give the sort chips a `radiogroup` semantic instead of three independent buttons** — `accessibilityState={{ selected }}` fixes the "which one is active" gap, but the group is still announced as three unrelated buttons. Wrapping the row in `accessibilityRole="radiogroup"` with `accessibilityRole="radio"` on each chip tells the screen reader these are mutually exclusive, which is what they actually are. Same one-prop-per-element cost.

- **Sweep the other chip rows for the same missing `selected` state** — `<ItemFilterBar>`'s quick-toggle chips, `app/chats.tsx`'s tab chips, the currency chips in `<CurrencySheet>` and the condition chips in `app/create.tsx` all use the same active/inactive-style pattern with no a11y state. A single pass plus one `chip-a11y-state.test.ts` scanning for `styles.*Active` used without a neighbouring `accessibilityState` would close the class and stop it reopening.

- **Extract `<SortChipRow filters onChange>` now that SORT_OPTIONS is shared** — the table moved out, but the ~20 lines of Pressable/Text/active-style chrome that consume it are still inline in the sheet. A component taking `{ value, onChange }` would let the future "sort all collections" sheet render the picker in one line instead of copying the chrome the table was extracted to protect.

- **Extend SORT_OPTIONS with the sorts users actually ask for** — the union is name-only today (`default` / `name-asc` / `name-desc`), yet the items carry `cost`, `acquiredAt` and `createdAt`, and the collection screen already computes totals from them. Adding `cost-asc`/`cost-desc`/`newest`/`oldest` rows is a table edit plus four `applySortMode` branches; the parity test already fails loudly if a union member ships without a UI row.

- **Persist the chosen sort per collection** — `itemFilters` resets to `EMPTY_FILTERS` on every mount, so a user who prefers A→Z re-picks it on every visit. Storing just the `sort` field under a `collectables-item-sort-v1-{collectionId}` key (the namespace the other prefs already use) is a small write, and it pairs with the reorder-mode persistence suggestion filed above — both are "the screen forgot what I chose" complaints.

### Palette luma helper + DANGER_* family pinning (2026-08-09)

- **Give the other numbered families their own `*-family.test.ts` now that the helper exists** — `MUTED_*` (29 shades), `CARD_BG_*` (15), `AMBER_*` (16) and `TEXT_ON_DARK_*` (13) each carry the same "the digits are IDs, not ranks" problem the DANGER family just had pinned, and none has a ranking snapshot. One ~40-line file per family (rank snapshot + no-duplicates + hue-dominance) reuses `rankByLuminance` / `findBandOverlaps` verbatim and would turn the four largest families from "trust the reviewer" into "the diff names the token that moved".

- **Add a real WCAG contrast helper alongside the Rec. 601 luma one** — `lib/color-luminance.ts` documents in its header that it must NOT be used for accessibility checks (no gamma expansion, so it cannot predict contrast ratios), which leaves the app with zero contrast coverage: nothing today asserts that `TEXT_ON_DARK_*` on `HERO_DARK_*`, or `MUTED_*` on `CARD_BG_*`, clears 4.5:1. A ~20-line `contrastRatio(fg, bg)` (sRGB → linear → relative luminance → ratio) plus a table test over the pairs that actually render together would catch the one class of palette bug the luma ordering deliberately cannot see.

- **Fold the DANGER band floor into `scripts/check-inline-hex.ts` as a palette-shape lint** — the band-separation assertion lives in a test, so it only runs under `npm test`; the hex guard runs in `lint:all` and is what contributors hit first. Teaching the existing script to also load `designTokens` and re-run `findBandOverlaps` over the soft/deep split would surface a bad shade at lint time, in the same output where the "route this through a named token" message already appears.

- **Sort each family light → dark in `lib/design-tokens.ts` source order and pin it** — the ranking snapshot proves the family is unordered on the page: `DANGER_SOFT_5` (the lightest red) is declared eleven lines below `DANGER_DEEP_5` (nearly the darkest). Reordering the declarations to match the ranking is a pure move-lines diff with no value changes, and `app/_dev/tokens.tsx` groups by prefix so the preview route would start reading as a ramp for free. The cool family already pins its source order this way.

- **Show the luma number on each swatch in `app/_dev/tokens.tsx`** — the preview route already computes it (via `isLightHex`) to pick the label colour, then throws it away. Rendering it as a small caption, and sorting each prefix group by it, would make "which of these seven pale reds is the surface and which is the border" answerable by looking rather than by opening the palette file — the exact question the DANGER band split exists to answer and the reason the family drifted into 17 shades in the first place.

### Timestamp comparator consistency (2026-08-09)

- **Teach `lint:all` about inconsistent comparators, not just this one shape** — the sweep in `__tests__/sort-helpers.test.ts` matches a hardcoded list of timestamp field names (`createdAt`, `soldAt`, `lastMessageAt`, `recordedAt`, …), so a new field sorted with the same ternary slips through until someone adds it to the regex. A `scripts/check-comparators.ts` that flags any `.sort()` callback whose body has no path returning `0` would catch the class rather than the instances, and would run in the guard suite where contributors see it first.

- **Property-test the comparators instead of enumerating cases** — the three contract rules (antisymmetry, transitivity, reflexivity-as-zero) are exactly what a `fast-check` property covers in three lines each, and would have found this bug on day one. It is one dev-dep, and it would apply equally to `applyItemFilters`' four-way sort in `lib/item-filters.ts`, which has the most branches of any comparator in the repo and only case-by-case coverage.

- **Audit `lib/item-filters.ts`'s title tiebreak for the same latent issue** — it is the one comparator that already handles ties correctly, via `collator.compare(a.title, b.title)`, but two items with the same title and the same sort key still return `0`, which is right, while two items with the same title under a *different* locale may not. Pinning the collator's options (`sensitivity`, `numeric`) rather than taking the default would make the sort reproducible across devices; today a Russian and an English locale can order the same collection differently.

- **Sort marketplace price history by `recordedAt` through `bySoldAtDesc` too** — `priceHistory` builds `recordedAt: l.soldAt ?? l.createdAt` and then sorts on the derived field, which duplicates the fallback the shared comparator already encodes. Having the builder sort the listings first and map second would let it reuse `bySoldAtDesc` and delete the third copy of the fallback expression, which the adoption test currently has to carve an exception for.

- **Give `ChatPreview` a stable secondary sort** — previews sort by `lastMessageAt` descending, which is now tie-consistent, but two chats whose last message landed in the same millisecond fall back to object-key insertion order from `Object.entries(messagesByChat)`. That is stable within a session and arbitrary across a cache reload. Adding `|| compareKeysAsc(a.chatId, b.chatId)` makes the list identical on every device for the same data, which matters because the chat list is the one screen users scan by position.

### <SoftDestructiveChip> extraction (2026-08-09)

- **Decide the collection delete button's shape and let it adopt a widget** — `app/collection/[id].tsx` is the last file importing `CARD_BG_10` + `DANGER_SOFT_2` + `DANGER_DEEP_4` straight from the palette, only because its delete button uses `RADIUS_CARD` where `<DangerSection tone="soft">` uses `RADIUS_PILL`. That is one design call — pill or card — and once it is made the screen drops three imports and two stylesheet entries, and the adoption sweep goes to zero exceptions instead of one named holdout.

- **Give `<DangerSection>` a `radius` prop instead, if the two shapes are both wanted** — the alternative to the above: the soft tone already varies by tone, and adding `shape?: "pill" | "card"` would let the collection screen adopt the widget without a visual change. Cheaper than a design decision, at the cost of one more axis on a component that already has five props.

- **Adopt `<SoftDestructiveChip>` for the sort and query clear-chips too** — `<ItemFilterBar>` renders three chips in the same row and only the reset one is now a component; the sort and query chips are the identical icon+label+dismiss shape in the dark `HERO_DARK` treatment rather than the pale red. A `tone` prop (`"destructive" | "neutral"`) would collapse all three into one widget and delete ~30 lines of near-duplicate chrome, which is what the row was heading toward anyway.

- **Pin the chip row's horizontal scroll behaviour** — the chips live in a horizontal `ScrollView` and the query chip is `numberOfLines={1}` specifically so a long needle cannot push the reset chip off the end where nothing hints it exists. That is a real constraint held only by a comment; a test asserting the query chip stays single-line and that reset renders last would keep a future reorder from re-opening it.

- **Extract the `<DangerIconButton />` sibling that the wishlist suggestions already filed** — with the chip and the block button both routed through `lib/danger-surface.ts`, the icon-only round trash button in `app/wishlist.tsx` is the third form of the same treatment and the only one still assembling it by hand. It is the smallest of the three and would complete the set, giving the danger family a chip / button / icon-button trio that all derive one surface.
